    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016Fw6Jj9ANiTAwnbDay55tV`</summary>

```text
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early; \citet{byerly2026selfconsistency} further analyze SC's limitations in mathematical reasoning. Alternative aggregation strategies beyond majority voting \citep{ai2025beyondmajority} have been proposed but do not address the underlying correlation problem. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	We distinguish three paradigms. \emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors even at this level. \emph{Program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \citet{kuai2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\kappa$ and $K_\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.
```

</details>

Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail."
        ]
      }
    ],
    "userModified": false,
    "replaceAll": false
  },

[Director 指令] [Patch] Oracle 重算级联更新 — Qwen14B PW Oracle 86.67→91.17%

exp032 canonical traces 重跑 Oracle 结果：91.17% (547/600)，比旧值 86.67% 高 4.5pp。Oracle Δ = 91.17 - 74.00 = **+17.17pp**。

**全文需更新的位置**（搜 12.67 和 86.67）：

### 1. experiments.tex
- Main Table Oracle 行：Oracle accuracy 86.67→**91.17**, Δ +12.67→**+17.17**
- §4.3 Oracle 正文段落：所有引用 +12.67 的地方改为 +17.17

### 2. abstract.tex

### 3. related_work.tex

### 4. diagnosis.tex (appendix 副本)
- Oracle 行同步更新

- Oracle subtype 表 Overall 行更新

### 叙事不变

编译确认 8 页。全文 grep 确认无 86.67 和 12.67 残留（注意 12.67 可能出现在非 Oracle 上下文，只改 Oracle 相关的）。

non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). The $\\pm 2$\\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \\citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\\,pp); $\\pm 1$\\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\\pm 3$\\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\\pm 2.4\\%$ relative at 85\\%) than low ones ($\\pm 5.1\\%$ at 39\\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nReplacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+12.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory (unmarked: single-seed exploratory).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & 59.31 & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\\%$)}} \\\\\nQwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 74.00 & \\textbf{91.17} & $\\mathbf{+17.17}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). The $\\pm 2$\\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \\citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\\,pp); $\\pm 1$\\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\\pm 3$\\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\\pm 2.4\\%$ relative at 85\\%) than low ones ($\\pm 5.1\\%$ at 39\\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nReplacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+12.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling.\n\n\n",

    "newString": "$+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter",
    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find no evidence of improvement across six open-source models (7B--32B), with equivalence established in four conditions, significant degradation in two (both in the lowest-accuracy regime), and ten conditions remaining underpowered (none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$).\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+12.67$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator (Mistral-7B $+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$, $w{=}3$), with combo gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap across six verifier-generator conditions ($p = 0.003$).\nWe further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% of verification cost across two held-out datasets.\n",

          "-Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+12.67$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.",

          " Cross-architecture NLI verification yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator (Mistral-7B $+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$, $w{=}3$), with combo gain monotonically ordered by the capability gap across six verifier-generator conditions ($p = 0.003$).",

    "newString": "$+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter)",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early; \\citet{byerly2026selfconsistency} further analyze SC's limitations in mathematical reasoning. Alternative aggregation strategies beyond majority voting \\citep{ai2025beyondmajority} have been proposed but do not address the underlying correlation problem. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms. \\emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors even at this level. \\emph{Program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+12.67$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \\citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \\citet{kuai2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\\kappa$ and $K_\\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.",

The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). The $\\pm 2$\\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \\citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\\,pp); $\\pm 1$\\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\\pm 3$\\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\\pm 2.4\\%$ relative at 85\\%) than low ones ($\\pm 5.1\\%$ at 39\\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps. Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). [Director 指令] [Patch] R11 补充分析批量整合 — 3 项进 Appendix

三个 R11 审稿人要求的 computation 分析已完成，全部进 Appendix 作为 robustness/supplementary evidence。Main text 只加最小必要的引用。

### 1. Relative TOST (Appendix, 新 section 或加入现有 TOST appendix)

R11 质疑 absolute ±2pp margin 在高 SC baseline 下不公平。Relative ±5% SC 结果：

| Margin | Equivalent | Different | Underpowered |
|--------|-----------|-----------|-------------|
| Abs ±2pp (primary) | 4 | 2 | 10 |
| Rel ±5% SC | 8 | 2 | 6 |

在 experiments.tex line 64 的现有 caveat（"This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines..."）后加括注：`(Appendix~\ref{app:relative_tost})`

### 2. SBERT Process-κ Robustness (Appendix, ~3 行)

R11 问 TF-IDF Process-κ 是否受表示方法影响。SBERT 结果更强化：

"Replacing TF-IDF with SBERT embeddings (all-MiniLM-L6-v2) yields ProcessSim = 0.91 (vs.\ TF-IDF 0.74), widening the gap over answer-level $\kappa = 0.55$. The process homogeneity finding is robust to representation choice."

加入现有 Process-κ appendix section。

### 3. 3-Verifier BH Summary (Appendix, 新表)

R11 问多重检验后 NLI combo 是否仍显著。BH correction m=15 结果：10/15 significant at α=0.05。

简明表格（5 verifier configs × 3 weights = 15 tests），标记 BH-significant 的 cells。

**注意**：main text BH 用 m=12（cross-architecture NLI），appendix 用 m=15（含 ensemble），需在 appendix 表注中说明。

不在 main text 提及 additive ensemble +12pp（8 页太紧，且需更多分析支撑）。

编译确认 8 页。

714	\section{TOST Equivalence Tests}
715	\label{app:tost}
716	
717	We apply two one-sided tests (TOST) \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with equivalence margin $\pm 2$\,pp to all 16 non-degenerate SICA--SC comparisons, classifying each condition as \emph{equivalent} (TOST significant), \emph{genuinely different} (McNemar $p < 0.05$ and TOST not significant), or \emph{underpowered} (neither test significant). Four conditions are equivalent, confirming that SICA and SC produce statistically indistinguishable accuracy. Two conditions show genuine differences, both negative: Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$). The remaining ten conditions are underpowered, reflecting the limited statistical power at $n{=}200$--$204$ for detecting $|\Delta| \leq 2$\,pp effects.
718	
719	\paragraph{Margin sensitivity.} Table~\ref{tab:tost-sensitivity} shows that the classification is robust across margins: regardless of the chosen margin, no condition shows a significant positive SICA effect.
720

728	Margin & Equivalent & Different & Underpowered \\
729	\midrule

732	$\pm 3$\,pp & 10 & 0 & 6 \\
733	\bottomrule

735	\end{table}

655	
656	
657	\section{Cross-Model Process-Level Independence}
658	\label{app:cross-model-process}
659	
660	Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process} extends the process-level independence analysis (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}) to four model families on FOLIO ($n = 204$). Mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ in every family, but the gap is model-strength dependent: it narrows from $0.31$ (LLaMA) and $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). For weaker models ($r \approx 0$), answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity---process-convergent but answer-divergent trace pairs constitute 9.5\% of all pairs on Mistral-7B versus 2.5--3.2\% on other families. For stronger models ($r \approx 0.23$, $p \leq 0.001$), process and answer agreement align, and answer-level $\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck. Process-$\kappa$ thus provides a crucial additional diagnostic specifically for weaker models, where answer-level metrics underestimate reasoning homogeneity.
661	
662	\begin{table}[h]
663	\centering
664	\small
665	\caption{Cross-model process-level independence on FOLIO ($n = 204$, $K = 12$). Mean Cosine: mean pairwise TF-IDF cosine similarity of reasoning traces (Eq.~\ref{eq:process-sim}). Answer $\kappa$: per-question answer agreement. Gap: Cosine $-$ $\kappa$. $r$: Pearson correlation between ProcessSim and answer agreement. Conv.: fraction of process-convergent but answer-divergent trace pairs. {*}{*}\,$p < 0.01$; {*}{*}{*}\,$p < 0.001$.}
666	\label{tab:cross-model-process}
667	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccrlr@{}}
668	\toprule
669	Model & Mean Cos. & Ans.\ $\kappa$ & Gap & $r$ & $p$ & Conv.\ (\%) \\
670	\midrule
671	Mistral-7B & 0.690 & 0.460 & 0.230 & $-$0.064 & 0.362 & 9.5 \\
672	LLaMA-3.1-8B & 0.759 & 0.447 & 0.312 & $-$0.001 & 0.985 & 3.2 \\
673	Qwen2.5-7B & 0.790 & 0.658 & 0.132 & $+$0.226 & 0.001{*}{*} & 2.8 \\
674	Qwen2.5-14B & 0.766 & 0.744 & 0.022 & $+$0.244 & ${<}$0.001{*}{*}{*} & 2.5 \\
675	\bottomrule
676	\end{tabular}
677	\end{table}
678	
679	
680	\section{Constraint Quality Across Methods}
681	\label{app:constraint-quality}
682	
683	Table~\ref{tab:constraint_quality} compares constraint quality metrics across the three method-level interventions on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Standard SICA extracts from reasoning traces and achieves the highest parse rate (97.1\%) but also the highest redundancy (42.9\%): nearly half of all constraints are syntactic duplicates across the 12 traces, quantifying the co-sourcing effect of same-model generation. ICG generates constraints directly from the problem statement without trace conditioning. Its lower parse rate (74.6\%) reflects the difficulty of standalone formalization at the 7B scale, though successfully parsed constraints compile at a higher rate (99.4\% vs.\ 96.5\%). ICG produces fewer unique constraints per question (24.9 vs.\ 39.3) with lower redundancy (32.3\%), consistent with the absence of trace-level repetition. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraint sets for each candidate answer. The format yields far fewer unique constraints per question (9.2) with zero redundancy (each pro/con pair is structurally distinct), but 62.7\% of constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3---they impose no logical tension and cannot discriminate between candidates. Only 51.0\% of Contrastive constraints are discriminative, compared to 81.4\% for standard SICA and 77.0\% for ICG.
684	
685	\begin{table}[h]
686	\centering
687	\small
688	\caption{Constraint quality metrics across method-level interventions (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n = 204$). Parse: fraction of extraction attempts yielding valid JSON. Z3 Compile: fraction compiling to Z3 formulas. Unique/q: mean unique constraints per question. Redundancy: fraction of constraints duplicated across traces. Disc.: fraction discriminative (different Z3 outcome per candidate). Triv.\ Sat.: fraction trivially satisfied (no logical tension).}

Only conditions where the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC accuracy show significant improvement, confirming Finding~2. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) survive.

64	\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\pm 2$\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). The $\pm 2$\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\,pp); $\pm 1$\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\pm 3$\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\pm 2.4\%$ relative at 85\%) than low ones ($\pm 5.1\%$ at 39\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps. We trace the failure below.

      "content": "\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). The $\\pm 2$\\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \\citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\\,pp); $\\pm 1$\\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\\pm 3$\\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\\pm 2.4\\%$ relative at 85\\%) than low ones ($\\pm 5.1\\%$ at 39\\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}",

[Director 指令] [Patch] E5 CIL Framework 数据整合

在 §3 或 §5 中加入 CIL 分析段落 + 表格。

**CIL 定义**：CIL(V,G) = log₂[P(V correct | G wrong) / P(V correct | G correct)]

**数据表格（8 pairs）**：

| Generator | Verifier | Dataset | CIL (bits) | Combo Gain (w=3) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mistral-7B | BART-MNLI | PW-600 | +1.86 | +5.17pp |
| Mistral-7B | DeBERTa-MNLI | PW-600 | +1.67 | +6.67pp |
| Mistral-7B | RoBERTa-MNLI | PW-600 | +1.42 | +4.17pp |
| Qwen2.5-14B | DeBERTa-MNLI | PW-600 | -0.08 | +0.83pp |
| Mistral-7B | DeBERTa-MNLI | FOLIO-204 | -0.47 | +4.41pp |
| Mistral-7B | SICA | FOLIO-204 | -2.93 | +4.90pp |
| Qwen2.5-14B | SICA | PW-600 | -3.53 | +0.50pp |
| Mistral-7B | SICA | PW-600 | -4.61 | -2.83pp |

**统计**：Spearman ρ = 0.762 (p=0.028), Pearson r = 0.734 (p=0.038)

**叙事要点**：
1. CIL > 0（cross-architecture NLI）→ verifier provides complementary signal when generator errs → combo gain
2. CIL < 0（SICA）→ same-model extraction amplifies errors → no gain or loss
3. CIL → 0 as generator strengthens → explains Aggregation Ceiling monotonic decay
4. CIL unifies SCB, NLI Escape, and Aggregation Ceiling under one information-theoretic metric

**注意**：FOLIO DeBERTa 有一个 anomaly（CIL=-0.47 but gain=+4.41pp），小样本效应（n_wrong=93），可以用脚注解释。

数据文件在 artifacts/ 下（Worker 已保存到服务器 results/exp_d134_cil/）。

这次不用验证引用。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019CnGNyTyjmp57PqVZqv8ba`</summary>

```text
205	\paragraph{Cumulative Information Lift.}
206	We define \textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL) to unify our diagnostics under a single information-theoretic quantity:
207	\begin{equation}
208	\label{eq:cil}
209	\text{CIL}(V, G) = \log \frac{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ wrong})}{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ correct})}.
210	\end{equation}
211	$\text{CIL} > 0$ means $V$ preferentially corrects $G$'s errors; $\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. CIL subsumes our findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\text{CIL}(\text{self}, G) \approx 0$; (2)~Finding~2 predicts $\text{CIL}(V, G) > 0$ when $V$ is independent and capable; (3)~DRC routes to $V$ where expected CIL is highest.
212	
213	\paragraph{Empirical CIL across verifier--generator pairs.}
214	Table~\ref{tab:cil_empirical} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator--dataset conditions, sorted by CIL descending. All three cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\text{CIL} > 0$ (verifier preferentially corrects generator errors), while all three same-model SICA conditions yield $\text{CIL} < 0$ (shared bias). CIL strongly predicts combo gain: Spearman $\rho = 0.881$ ($p = 0.004$), Pearson $r = 0.854$ ($p = 0.008$).
215	
216	\begin{table}[h]
217	\centering
218	\small
219	\caption{Empirical CIL across eight verifier--generator conditions (sorted by CIL descending). $\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-architecture NLI verifiers on ProofWriter. $\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or mismatched capability gap.}
220	\label{tab:cil_empirical}
221	\begin{tabular}{@{}lllrr@{}}
222	\toprule
223	Generator & Verifier & Dataset & CIL (bits) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
224	\midrule
225	Mistral-7B & BART-MNLI & PW-600 & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\
226	Mistral-7B & DeBERTa-MNLI & PW-600 & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\

      "content": "\\paragraph{Cumulative Information Lift.}\nWe define \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL) to unify our diagnostics under a single information-theoretic quantity:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ means $V$ preferentially corrects $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. CIL subsumes our findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\approx 0$; (2)~Finding~2 predicts $\\text{CIL}(V, G) > 0$ when $V$ is independent and capable; (3)~DRC routes to $V$ where expected CIL is highest.\n\n\\paragraph{Empirical CIL across verifier--generator pairs.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:cil_empirical} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator--dataset conditions, sorted by CIL descending. All three cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$ (verifier preferentially corrects generator errors), while all three same-model SICA conditions yield $\\text{CIL} < 0$ (shared bias). CIL strongly predicts combo gain: Spearman $\\rho = 0.881$ ($p = 0.004$), Pearson $r = 0.854$ ($p = 0.008$).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Empirical CIL across eight verifier--generator conditions (sorted by CIL descending). $\\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-architecture NLI verifiers on ProofWriter. $\\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or mismatched capability gap.}\n\\label{tab:cil_empirical}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lllrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Verifier & Dataset & CIL (bits) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & BART-MNLI & PW-600 & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\\\\nMistral-7B & DeBERTa-MNLI & PW-600 & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\\\\nMistral-7B & RoBERTa-MNLI & PW-600 & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & DeBERTa-MNLI & PW-600 & $-$0.21 & $+$0.33 \\\\\nMistral-7B & DeBERTa-MNLI & FOLIO-204 & $-$0.47 & $+$2.94 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B & SICA & FOLIO-204 & $-$2.93 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & SICA & PW-600 & $-$4.21 & $-$2.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B & SICA & PW-600 & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Per-condition diagnostics.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:capability_trap} reports per-condition diagnostic metrics referenced in \\S\\ref{sec:entropy}.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small",

60	
61	
62	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
63	\label{sec:diagnostics}
64	
65	Further diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.
66	
67	
68	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
69	\label{sec:remediation}
70	\label{sec:interventions}
71	
72	\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \ref{tab:remediation}, \ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.
73	
74	\begin{figure*}[t]
75	\centering
76	\includegraphics[width=0.85\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}
77	\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
78	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
79	\end{figure*}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}",
  "new_string": "\\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil-main}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log_2 \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL predicts combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = 0.762$, $p = 0.028$; Pearson $r = 0.734$, $p = 0.038$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.\\footnote{One anomaly: Mistral-7B $\\times$ DeBERTa on FOLIO shows $\\text{CIL} = -0.47$ yet $\\Delta = +4.41$\\,pp; small error count ($n_{\\text{wrong}} = 93$) amplifies sampling variance.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}\n\\label{tab:cil_main}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral $\\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen-14B $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.08 & $+$0.83 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$4.41 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$4.90 \\\\\nQwen-14B $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$3.53 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}"
}

    "newString": "\\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil-main}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log_2 \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL predicts combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = 0.762$, $p = 0.028$; Pearson $r = 0.734$, $p = 0.038$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.\\footnote{One anomaly: Mistral-7B $\\times$ DeBERTa on FOLIO shows $\\text{CIL} = -0.47$ yet $\\Delta = +4.41$\\,pp; small error count ($n_{\\text{wrong}} = 93$) amplifies sampling variance.}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}\n\\label{tab:cil_main}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral $\\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen-14B $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.08 & $+$0.83 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$4.41 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$4.90 \\\\\nQwen-14B $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$3.53 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. First, BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Second, removing the answer sentence from CoT traces before extraction yields no BR decrease across three truncation strategies (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$, smallest $p = 0.12$, conservative truncation; per-problem mean BR: full $2.79$, truncated $2.26$--$2.66$, computed over problems with finite ratio) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB is consistent with a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",
